     6
     7	# DDD Architecture
     8
     9	Use this skill to turn business language and state changes into domain boundaries, model candidates, invariants, and handoffs. DDD here starts with strategic design before tactical patterns.
    10
    11	## Routing
    12
    13	- If the user explicitly asks for subagents, subagent/subagents, 서브에이전트, 역할 분해, 병렬 검토, 책임 분배, or dddjango workflow in a Django task, use `workflow-dddjango-subagents` first.
    14	- If the work is composite or risky across domain rules, DB schema/transactions, REST contracts, Django implementation, and tests, use `workflow-dddjango-subagents` first.
    15	- If the question is mainly DB schema, constraints, indexes, transaction isolation, locking, or rollout risk, use `architecture-db`; use this skill first only when the domain invariant or aggregate boundary is unclear.
    16	- If the question is mainly REST resources, URL shape, status codes, Problem Details, pagination, idempotency, or OpenAPI, use `architecture-api`; use this skill first only when domain terms or use cases are unclear.
    17	- If the question is mainly layered/clean/hexagonal structure, ports/adapters, repository/UoW implementation, CQRS, outbox, ACL, or dependency direction, use `architecture-implementation-patterns` after the DDD model is clear.
    18	- If the user asks for Django models, migrations, routers, views, templates, or tests and the domain rules are already clear, use the relevant implementation skill directly.
    19	- For simple CRUD or a tiny wording explanation with no meaningful domain policy, do not force aggregates, context maps, or event storming.
    20
    21	## Reference Loading
    22
    23	- Read [strategic-design.md](references/strategic-design.md) for subdomain classification, problem vs solution space, ubiquitous language, bounded contexts, distillation, event storming, and team topology.
    24	- Read [context-map.md](references/context-map.md) for context relationships, upstream/downstream direction, Partnership, Shared Kernel, Customer-Supplier, Conformist, ACL, OHS, Published Language, and Separated Ways.
    25	- Read [tactical-patterns.md](references/tactical-patterns.md) for value objects, entities, aggregates, repositories as aggregate persistence concepts, domain services, application services, specifications, and supple design.
    26	- Read [domain-events.md](references/domain-events.md) for domain events, dispatch timing, consistency boundaries, outbox decision points, event sourcing, saga, and integration-event boundaries.
    27
    28	## Runtime Rules
    29
    30	- Start with strategic design: subdomains, bounded contexts, ubiquitous language, and context relationships before applying tactical patterns.
    31	- Separate problem space from solution space: discover domains and subdomains; design bounded contexts and model boundaries.
    32	- Classify core, supporting, and generic subdomains so implementation strength matches business value and complexity.
    33	- Keep ubiquitous language scoped to a bounded context. Same words may mean different things in different contexts.
    34	- Design aggregates around true invariants and consistency boundaries. Keep them small, expose behavior through the root, and reference other aggregates by ID.
    35	- Prefer real domain behavior in entities and value objects over anemic data structures plus procedural services.
    36	- Use domain services for stateless domain rules that do not naturally belong to one aggregate; application services orchestrate use cases and transactions without owning business rules.
    37	- When mapping toward Django, do not require a separate pure domain model by default. Django models can carry simple domain behavior; separate domain and infrastructure when ORM lifecycle, lazy loading, HTTP, SDKs, or framework details obscure the rules.
    38	- If the target design puts core business rules in a Router, view, template, serializer/schema, or other adapter, identify it as a boundary problem and move the rule to domain or application responsibility before implementation.
    39	- When raising domain events, state what happened, who consumes it if known, and whether dispatch happens before commit, after commit, or through an outbox-style handoff.
    40	- For risky writes, state the DDD-owned invariant, aggregate boundary, consistency boundary, and event or side-effect timing, then assign transaction ownership, locking, idempotency storage, `Idempotency-Key` behavior, isolation/retry, and integration/concurrency test criteria to workflow, DB, API, and Test responsibilities unless the current step explicitly owns those decisions.
    41	- Return concrete design decisions: subdomain type, bounded context, context-map relationship, key terms, aggregate candidates, invariants, events, consistency boundary, and use case candidates.
    42	- Do not write Django model, Router, migration, or test code during DDD-only modeling. In combined work, make the DDD decisions first and hand implementation to the relevant skills or assigned implementation step.
    43	- Only report tests, validation, review, browser checks, or subagent work that was actually executed. If not executed, say so.
